</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Time zone</th><td class="infobox-data"><span class="nowrap"><a href="/wiki/Coordinated_Universal_Time" title="Coordinated Universal Time">UTC</a>+2</span> (<a href="/wiki/South_African_Standard_Time" title="South African Standard Time">SAST</a>)</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Date format</th><td class="infobox-data">dd/mm/yyyy</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Left-_and_right-hand_traffic" title="Left- and right-hand traffic">Driving side</a></th><td class="infobox-data">left</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Telephone_numbers_in_Eswatini" title="Telephone numbers in Eswatini">Calling code</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/%2B268" class="mw-redirect" title="+268">+268</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166" title="ISO 3166">ISO 3166 code</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-2:SZ" title="ISO 3166-2:SZ">SZ</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Country_code_top-level_domain" title="Country code top-level domain">Internet TLD</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/.sz" title=".sz">.sz</a></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data"><div class="ib-country-website"><b>Website</b><br /><span class="url"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.gov.sz/">www<wbr />.gov<wbr />.sz</a></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Eswatini</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˌ/: secondary stress follows">ˌ</span><span title="/ɛ/: &#39;e&#39; in &#39;dress&#39;">ɛ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="&#39;w&#39; in &#39;wind&#39;">w</span><span title="/ɑː/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;father&#39;">ɑː</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;t&#39; in &#39;tie&#39;">t</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/i/: &#39;y&#39; in &#39;happy&#39;">i</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">ESS</span>-wah-<span style="font-size:90%">TEE</span>-nee</i></a>; <a href="/wiki/Swazi_language" title="Swazi language">Swazi</a>: <i lang="ss">eSwatini</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="ss-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Nguni" title="Help:IPA/Nguni">[ɛswáˈtʼiːni]</a></span>), officially the <b>Kingdom of Eswatini</b> and formerly named <b>Swaziland</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="&#39;w&#39; in &#39;wind&#39;">w</span><span title="/ɑː/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;father&#39;">ɑː</span><span title="&#39;z&#39; in &#39;zoom&#39;">z</span><span title="/i/: &#39;y&#39; in &#39;happy&#39;">i</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/æ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;bad&#39;">æ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="&#39;d&#39; in &#39;dye&#39;">d</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">SWAH</span>-zee-land</i></a>; officially renamed in 2018),<sup id="cite_ref-:2_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-KingdEswatini_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KingdEswatini-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> is a <a href="/wiki/Landlocked_country" title="Landlocked country">landlocked country</a> in <a href="/wiki/Southern_Africa" title="Southern Africa">Southern Africa</a>. It is bordered by <a href="/wiki/Mozambique" title="Mozambique">Mozambique</a> to its northeast and <a href="/wiki/South_Africa" title="South Africa">South Africa</a> to its north, west, south, and southeast. At no more than 200&#160;km (120&#160;mi) north to south and 130&#160;km (81&#160;mi) east to west, Eswatini is one of the smallest countries in Africa; despite this, its climate and topography are diverse, ranging from a cool and mountainous <a href="/wiki/Highveld" title="Highveld">highveld</a> to a hot and dry <a href="/wiki/Veld" title="Veld">lowveld</a>.
</p><p>The population is composed primarily of ethnic <a href="/wiki/Swazi_people" title="Swazi people">Swazis</a>. The prevalent language is <a href="/wiki/Swazi_language" title="Swazi language">Swazi</a> (<i>siSwati</i> in native form). The Swazis established their kingdom in the mid-18th century under the leadership of <a href="/wiki/Ngwane_III" title="Ngwane III">Ngwane III</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Bonner82_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bonner82-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> The country and the Swazi take their names from <a href="/wiki/Mswati_II" title="Mswati II">Mswati II</a>, the 19th-century king under whose rule the country was expanded and unified; its boundaries were drawn up in 1881 in the midst of the <a href="/wiki/Scramble_for_Africa" title="Scramble for Africa">Scramble for Africa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Kuper86_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kuper86-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> After the <a href="/wiki/Second_Boer_War" title="Second Boer War">Second Boer War</a>, the kingdom, under the name of Swaziland, was a British <a href="/wiki/High_Commissioner_for_Southern_Africa" title="High Commissioner for Southern Africa">high commission territory</a> from 1903 until it regained its full independence on 6 September 1968.<sup id="cite_ref-Gillis99_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gillis99-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> In April 2018, the official name was changed from Kingdom of Swaziland to Kingdom of Eswatini, mirroring the name commonly used in Swazi.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-KingdEswatini_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KingdEswatini-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The government is an <a href="/wiki/Absolute_monarchy" title="Absolute monarchy">absolute monarchy</a>, the last of its kind in Africa,<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> and has been ruled by King <a href="/wiki/Mswati_III" title="Mswati III">Mswati III</a> since 1986.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Elections are held every five years to determine the House of Assembly and the Senate majority. Its <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Eswatini" title="Constitution of Eswatini">constitution</a> was adopted in 2005. <i><a href="/wiki/Umhlanga_(ceremony)" title="Umhlanga (ceremony)">Umhlanga</a></i>, the reed dance held in August/September,<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> and <i><a href="/wiki/Incwala" title="Incwala">incwala</a></i>, the kingship dance held in December/January, are the nation's most important events.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Eswatini<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> is a <a href="/wiki/Developing_country" title="Developing country">developing country</a> and is classified as a lower-middle income economy. As a member of the <a href="/wiki/Southern_African_Customs_Union" title="Southern African Customs Union">Southern African Customs Union</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Common_Market_for_Eastern_and_Southern_Africa" title="Common Market for Eastern and Southern Africa">Common Market for Eastern and Southern Africa</a>, its main local trading partner is South Africa; to ensure economic stability, Eswatini's currency, the <a href="/wiki/Swazi_lilangeni" title="Swazi lilangeni">lilangeni</a>, is <a href="/wiki/Fixed_exchange_rate_system" title="Fixed exchange rate system">pegged</a> to the <a href="/wiki/South_African_rand" title="South African rand">South African rand</a>. Eswatini's major overseas trading partners are the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a><sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The majority of the country's employment is provided by its agricultural and manufacturing sectors. Eswatini is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Southern_African_Development_Community" title="Southern African Development Community">Southern African Development Community</a>, the <a href="/wiki/African_Union" title="African Union">African Union</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth of Nations</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>.
</p><p>The Swazi population faces major health issues: <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS" title="HIV/AIDS">HIV/AIDS</a> and (to a lesser extent) <a href="/wiki/Tuberculosis" title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a> are widespread.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> 28% of the adult population is HIV-positive.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> As of 2018, Eswatini has the 12th-lowest <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_life_expectancy" title="List of countries by life expectancy">life expectancy in the world</a>, at 58 years.<sup id="cite_ref-Economist_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Economist-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> The population of Eswatini is young. As of 2018, people aged 14 years or younger constitute 35% of the country's population and the median age is 22 years.<sup id="cite_ref-Demo_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Demo-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Eswatini" title="History of Eswatini">History of Eswatini</a></div>
<p>Artefacts have been found indicating human activity dating back to the early <a href="/wiki/Stone_Age" title="Stone Age">Stone Age</a>, around 200,000 years ago. Prehistoric <a href="/wiki/Rock_art" title="Rock art">rock art</a> paintings dating from as far back as c. 27,000 years ago to as recently as the 19th century can be found around the country.<sup id="cite_ref-South_African_History_Online_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-South_African_History_Online-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The earliest known inhabitants of the region were <a href="/wiki/Khoisan" title="Khoisan">Khoisan</a> <a href="/wiki/Hunter-gatherer" title="Hunter-gatherer">hunter-gatherers</a>. They were largely replaced by the <a href="/wiki/Nguni_people" title="Nguni people">Nguni</a> during the great <a href="/wiki/Bantu_expansion" title="Bantu expansion">Bantu migrations</a>. These peoples originated from the <a href="/wiki/African_Great_Lakes" title="African Great Lakes">Great Lakes region</a> of eastern and central Africa. Evidence of agriculture and iron use dates from about the 4th century. People speaking languages ancestral to the current <a href="/wiki/Sotho%E2%80%93Tswana_languages" title="Sotho–Tswana languages">Sotho</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nguni_languages" title="Nguni languages">Nguni languages</a> began settling no later than the 11th century.<sup id="cite_ref-BonnerPhilip_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BonnerPhilip-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Swazi_settlers_.2818th_and_19th_centuries.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Swazi_settlers_(18th_and_19th_centuries)">Swazi settlers (18th and 19th centuries)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Swazi settlers (18th and 19th centuries)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Swazi settlers, then known as <i>the Ngwane</i> (or <i>bakaNgwane</i>) before entering Eswatini, had been settled on the banks of the <a href="/wiki/Pongola_River" title="Pongola River">Pongola River</a>. Before that, they were settled in the area of the <a href="/wiki/Tembe_River" title="Tembe River">Tembe River</a> near present-day <a href="/wiki/Maputo" title="Maputo">Maputo</a>, Mozambique. Continuing conflict with the <a href="/wiki/Ndwandwe" title="Ndwandwe">Ndwandwe</a> people pushed them further north, with <a href="/wiki/Ngwane_III" title="Ngwane III">Ngwane III</a> establishing his capital at <a href="/wiki/Shiselweni_Region" title="Shiselweni Region">Shiselweni</a> at the foot of the Mhlosheni hills.<sup id="cite_ref-BonnerPhilip_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BonnerPhilip-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> Under <a href="/wiki/Sobhuza_I" title="Sobhuza I">Sobhuza I</a>, the Ngwane people established their capital at <a href="/wiki/Zombodze" title="Zombodze">Zombodze</a> in the heartland of present-day Eswatini. In this process, they conquered and incorporated the long established clans of the country known to the Swazi as <i>Emakhandzambili</i> (those found ahead).<sup id="cite_ref-BonnerPhilip_31-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BonnerPhilip-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:R%C3%A9cipient_Swazi.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/R%C3%A9cipient_Swazi.jpg/220px-R%C3%A9cipient_Swazi.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/R%C3%A9cipient_Swazi.jpg/330px-R%C3%A9cipient_Swazi.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/R%C3%A9cipient_Swazi.jpg/440px-R%C3%A9cipient_Swazi.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2736" data-file-height="3648" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:R%C3%A9cipient_Swazi.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A 19th-century Swazi container, carved in wood</div></div></div>
<p>Eswatini derives its name from a later king named <a href="/wiki/Mswati_II" title="Mswati II">Mswati II</a>. <i>KaNgwane</i>, named for Ngwane III, is an alternative name for Eswatini, the surname of whose <a href="/wiki/Dynasty" title="Dynasty">royal house</a> remains <i>Nkhosi <a href="/wiki/House_of_Dlamini" title="House of Dlamini">Dlamini</a></i>. <i>Nkhosi</i> literally means "king". Mswati II was the greatest of the fighting kings of Eswatini, and he greatly extended the area of the country to twice its current size. The <i>Emakhandzambili</i> clans were initially incorporated into the kingdom with wide autonomy, often including grants of special ritual and political status. The extent of their autonomy, however, was drastically curtailed by Mswati, who attacked and subdued some of them in the 1850s.<sup id="cite_ref-BonnerPhilip_31-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BonnerPhilip-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> With his power, Mswati greatly reduced the influence of the <i>Emakhandzambili</i> while incorporating more people into his kingdom either through conquest or by giving them refuge. These later arrivals became known to the Swazis as <i>Emafikamuva</i>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Johnston,_W._and_A.K._-_South_African_Republic._Orange_Free_State,_Natal,_Basuto_Land,_Etc..jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Johnston%2C_W._and_A.K._-_South_African_Republic._Orange_Free_State%2C_Natal%2C_Basuto_Land%2C_Etc..jpg/220px-Johnston%2C_W._and_A.K._-_South_African_Republic._Orange_Free_State%2C_Natal%2C_Basuto_Land%2C_Etc..jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="298" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Johnston%2C_W._and_A.K._-_South_African_Republic._Orange_Free_State%2C_Natal%2C_Basuto_Land%2C_Etc..jpg/330px-Johnston%2C_W._and_A.K._-_South_African_Republic._Orange_Free_State%2C_Natal%2C_Basuto_Land%2C_Etc..jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Johnston%2C_W._and_A.K._-_South_African_Republic._Orange_Free_State%2C_Natal%2C_Basuto_Land%2C_Etc..jpg/440px-Johnston%2C_W._and_A.K._-_South_African_Republic._Orange_Free_State%2C_Natal%2C_Basuto_Land%2C_Etc..jpg 2x" data-file-width="3152" data-file-height="4269" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Johnston,_W._and_A.K._-_South_African_Republic._Orange_Free_State,_Natal,_Basuto_Land,_Etc..jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Swaziland in Southern Africa, 1896</div></div></div>
<p>The autonomy of the Swazi nation was influenced by British and Dutch rule of southern Africa in the 19th and early 20th centuries. In 1881, the British government signed a convention recognising Swazi independence, despite the <a href="/wiki/Scramble_for_Africa" title="Scramble for Africa">Scramble for Africa</a> that was taking place at the time. This independence was also recognised in the <a href="/wiki/London_Convention_(1884)" title="London Convention (1884)">London Convention of 1884</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>King <a href="/wiki/Mbandzeni" title="Mbandzeni">Mbandzeni</a> created a complex pattern of land ownership by granting many concessions to Europeans. During the concessions some of the King's senior chiefs like Chief Ntengu Mbokane got the permission to relocate to farms towards the Lubombo region, in modern day city of Nsoko. Others like Mshiza Maseko relocated to farms towards the Komati River in the place called eLuvalweni. The concessions included grants and leases for agriculture and grazing. In 1890, following the death of Mbandzeni, a Swaziland Convention created a Chief Court to determine disputes about controversial land and mineral rights and other concessions.<sup id="cite_ref-CO_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CO-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Swaziland was given a <a href="/wiki/Triumvirate" title="Triumvirate">triumviral</a> administration in 1890, representing the British, the Dutch republics, and the Swazi people. In 1894, a convention placed Swaziland under the <a href="/wiki/South_African_Republic" title="South African Republic">South African Republic</a> as a <a href="/wiki/Protectorate" title="Protectorate">protectorate</a>. This continued under the rule of <a href="/wiki/Ngwane_V" title="Ngwane V">Ngwane V</a> until the outbreak of the <a href="/wiki/Second_Boer_War" title="Second Boer War">Second Boer War</a> in October 1899.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>King Ngwane V died in December 1899, during <a href="/wiki/Incwala" title="Incwala">incwala</a>, after the outbreak of the Second Boer War. His successor, <a href="/wiki/Sobhuza_II" title="Sobhuza II">Sobhuza II</a>, was four months old. Swaziland was indirectly involved in the war with various skirmishes between the British and the Boers occurring in the country until 1902.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="British_indirect_rule_over_Swaziland_.281906.E2.80.931968.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="British_indirect_rule_over_Swaziland_(1906–1968)">British indirect rule over Swaziland (1906–1968)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: British indirect rule over Swaziland (1906–1968)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1903, after the British victory in the Second Boer War, Swaziland became one of the British "<a href="/wiki/High_Commissioner_for_Southern_Africa" title="High Commissioner for Southern Africa">High Commission Territories</a>", the others being <a href="/wiki/Basutoland" title="Basutoland">Basutoland</a> (now <a href="/wiki/Lesotho" title="Lesotho">Lesotho</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Bechuanaland_Protectorate" title="Bechuanaland Protectorate">Bechuanaland</a>, although a protectorate was not established because terms had not been agreed with the Swazi Queen Regent <a href="/wiki/Labotsibeni_Mdluli" title="Labotsibeni Mdluli">Labotsibeni Mdluli</a>.
</p><p>The Swaziland Administration Proclamation of 1904 established a commission with the task of examining all the concessions and defining their boundaries. This work was finished by 1907, and the Swaziland Concessions Partition Proclamation provided for a concessions partition commissioner to be appointed to set aside areas for the sole use and occupation of the Swazis. The commissioner had the power to expropriate up to one third of each concession without compensation, but payment would need to be made if more than a third was taken. In the event, in 1910 he completed his work and set aside 1,639,687 acres, some 38% of Swaziland's area, for the Swazi. The queen regent then encouraged the Swazi to go to work in the Transvaal to earn money to buy more land from the Europeans.<sup id="cite_ref-CO_33-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CO-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Much of the early administration of the territory (for example, postal services) was carried out from South Africa until 1906, when the <a href="/wiki/Transvaal_Colony" title="Transvaal Colony">Transvaal Colony</a> was granted self-government. A British <a href="/wiki/High_commissioner" title="High commissioner">high commissioner</a> had some of the functions of a governor, but the Swazis were self-governing on their reserves, and the territory was not deemed to be a British possession.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> Sobhuza's official coronation as king was in December 1921 after the regency of Labotsibeni, after which he led an unsuccessful deputation to the <a href="/wiki/Judicial_Committee_of_the_Privy_Council" title="Judicial Committee of the Privy Council">Judicial Committee of the Privy Council</a> of the United Kingdom in London in 1922 regarding the issue of the land.<sup id="cite_ref-Vail91_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vail91-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the period between 1923 and 1963, Sobhuza II established the Swazi Commercial Amadoda which was to grant licences to small businesses on the Swazi reserves and also established the Swazi National School to counter the dominance of the missions in education. His stature grew with time, and the Swazi royal leadership was successful in resisting the weakening power of the British administration and the possibility of the incorporation of Swaziland into the <a href="/wiki/Union_of_South_Africa" title="Union of South Africa">Union of South Africa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Vail91_35-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vail91-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The constitution for independent Swaziland was promulgated by Britain in November 1963 under the terms of which legislative and executive councils were established. This development was opposed by the king's Swazi National Council (<i>Liqoqo</i>). Despite such opposition, elections took place, and the first <i>Legislative Council of Swaziland</i> was constituted on 9 September 1964.<sup id="cite_ref-profile_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-profile-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> By 1964, the area of the country reserved for occupation by the Swazi had increased to 56%.<sup id="cite_ref-CO_33-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CO-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Changes to the original constitution proposed by the Legislative Council were accepted by Britain and a new constitution providing for a House of Assembly and Senate was drawn up. Elections under this constitution were held in 1967.<sup id="cite_ref-profile_36-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-profile-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> Following the 1967 elections, Swaziland was a protected state until independence was regained in 1968.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Independence_.281968.E2.80.93present.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Independence_(1968–present)">Independence (1968–present)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Independence (1968–present)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Following the elections of 1973, the constitution of Swaziland was suspended by King Sobhuza II who thereafter ruled the country by <a href="/wiki/Decree" title="Decree">decree</a> until his death in 1982. At that point, Sobhuza II had been king of Swaziland for almost 83 years, making him the <a href="/wiki/List_of_longest-reigning_monarchs" title="List of longest-reigning monarchs">longest-reigning monarch in history</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> A regency followed his death, with Queen Regent <a href="/wiki/Dzeliwe_of_Eswatini" title="Dzeliwe of Eswatini">Dzeliwe Shongwe</a> as head of state until 1984 when she was removed by the Liqoqo and replaced by Queen Mother <a href="/wiki/Ntfombi_of_Eswatini" title="Ntfombi of Eswatini">Ntfombi Tfwala</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Mswati_III" title="Mswati III">Mswati III</a>, the son of Ntfombi, was crowned king in 1986 as king and <i><a href="/wiki/Ngwenyama" title="Ngwenyama">ngwenyama</a></i> of Swaziland.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>An attempt to transfer neighbouring parts of <a href="/wiki/South_Africa" title="South Africa">South Africa</a>, more precisely parts of the Zulu <a href="/wiki/Bantustan" title="Bantustan">homeland</a> of <a href="/wiki/KwaZulu" title="KwaZulu">KwaZulu</a> and parts of the Swazi homeland of <a href="/wiki/KaNgwane" title="KaNgwane">KaNgwane</a>, to Swaziland in 1982 was never realized.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> This would have given land-locked Swaziland access to the sea. The deal was negotiated by the governments of South Africa and Swaziland, but was met by popular opposition in the territory meant to be transferred.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> The territory had been claimed by Sobhuza II as part of the Swazi monarchs' traditional realm, and the South African government hoped to use the area as a buffer zone against guerrilla infiltration from <a href="/wiki/Mozambique" title="Mozambique">Mozambique</a>. (The South African government responded to the failure of the transfer by temporarily suspending the autonomy of KaNgwane.)<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 1990s saw a rise in student and labour protests calling on the king to introduce reforms.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Thus, progress towards constitutional reforms began, culminating with the introduction of the current Swazi constitution in 2005. This happened despite objections by political activists. The current constitution does not clearly deal with the status of political parties.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> The first election under the constitution took place in 2008. Members of Parliament (MPs) were elected from 55 constituencies (also known as <i>tinkhundla</i>). These MPs served five-year terms which ended in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_46-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, Swaziland suffered an economic crisis which was caused by reduced <a href="/wiki/Southern_African_Customs_Union" title="Southern African Customs Union">Southern African Customs Union</a> (SACU) receipts. This caused the government to request a loan from neighbouring South Africa. However, they did not agree with the conditions of the loan, which included political reforms.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During this period, there was increased pressure on the Swazi government to carry out more reforms. Public protests by civic organisations and <a href="/wiki/Trade_union" title="Trade union">trade unions</a> became more common. Starting in 2012, improvements in SACU receipts eased the fiscal pressure on the Swazi government. A new parliament, the second since the promulgation of the constitution, was elected in 2013. The king then reappointed <a href="/wiki/Barnabas_Sibusiso_Dlamini" title="Barnabas Sibusiso Dlamini">Sibusiso Dlamini</a> as prime minister for the third time.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 19 April 2018, Mswati III announced that the Kingdom of Swaziland had been renamed as the Kingdom of Eswatini, reflecting the extant Swazi name for the state <i>eSwatini</i>, to mark the 50th anniversary of Swazi independence. The name Eswatini means "land of the Swazis" in the Swazi language and was partially intended to prevent confusion with the similarly named <a href="/wiki/Switzerland" title="Switzerland">Switzerland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-KingdEswatini_11-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KingdEswatini-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Eswatini workers began anti-government protests against low salaries in September 2018. They went on a three-day strike organised by the <a href="/wiki/Trade_Union_Congress_of_Eswatini" title="Trade Union Congress of Eswatini">Trade Union Congress of Eswatini</a> that resulted in widespread disruption.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> In June 2021, <a href="/wiki/2021_Eswatini_protests" class="mw-redirect" title="2021 Eswatini protests">pro-democracy protests</a> broke out across the country, sparking riots, looting, and street skirmishes with police and soldiers. This civil unrest began as a result of years of anger towards the lack of meaningful reforms that would nudge Eswatini in the direction of democracy, as well as the government's reported banning of the submission of petitions. Numerous buildings said to be connected to King Mswati III were torched by protesters, and police reportedly assaulted and arrested political opponents. <i><a href="/wiki/The_New_York_Times" title="The New York Times">The New York Times</a></i> called the turmoil in the landlocked nation "the most explosive civil unrest in its 53&#160;years of independence".<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> At least 20 people were killed by state security forces and dozens more injured and detained. The government shut down the internet (with the compliance of mobile providers MTN and Eswatini Mobile) making it difficult to access reliable news from the country. The king was also said to have fled the country, though government officials disputed those claims, also calling for an end to the protests.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Eswatini" title="Geography of Eswatini">Geography of Eswatini</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:332px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Eswatini_Topography.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Eswatini_Topography.png/330px-Eswatini_Topography.png" decoding="async" width="330" height="327" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Eswatini_Topography.png/495px-Eswatini_Topography.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Eswatini_Topography.png/660px-Eswatini_Topography.png 2x" data-file-width="1425" data-file-height="1411" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Eswatini_Topography.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Topographic map of Eswatini</div></div></div>
<p>A small landlocked kingdom with an area of 17,364&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (6,704&#160;sq&#160;mi), Eswatini is located at approximately 26°30'S, 31°30'E<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> and is bordered in the north, west and south by South Africa and by Mozambique in the east. Along the eastern border with Mozambique is the <a href="/wiki/Lebombo_Mountains" title="Lebombo Mountains">Lebombo Mountains</a>, a mountain ridge at an altitude of around 600 metres (2,000&#160;ft). The mountains are broken by the <a href="/wiki/Canyon" title="Canyon">canyons</a> of three rivers, the <a href="/wiki/Ngwavuma_River" title="Ngwavuma River">Ngwavuma</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Maputo_River" title="Maputo River">Great Usutu</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Mbuluzi_River" title="Mbuluzi River">Mbuluzi</a>. The western border, with an average altitude of 1,200 metres (3,900&#160;ft), lies on the edge of an <a href="/wiki/Escarpment" title="Escarpment">escarpment</a>.
</p><p>Eswatini is separated into four geographical regions. These run from north to south and are determined by altitude. <a href="/wiki/Mbabane" title="Mbabane">Mbabane</a>, the capital, is on the <a href="/wiki/Highveld" title="Highveld">Highveld</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> The Middleveld, lying at an average 700 metres (2,300&#160;ft) above sea level is the most densely populated region of Eswatini with a lower rainfall than the mountains. <a href="/wiki/Manzini,_Eswatini" title="Manzini, Eswatini">Manzini</a>, the principal commercial and industrial city, is situated in the Middleveld.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The Lowveld, at around 250 metres (820&#160;ft), is less populated than other areas and presents a typical African bush country of thorn trees and grasslands. Eswatini contains three ecosystems: <a href="/wiki/Maputaland_coastal_forest_mosaic" title="Maputaland coastal forest mosaic">Maputaland coastal forest mosaic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zambezian_and_mopane_woodlands" title="Zambezian and mopane woodlands">Zambezian and mopane woodlands</a>, and Drakensberg <a href="/wiki/Montane_grasslands_and_shrublands" title="Montane grasslands and shrublands">montane grasslands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> The country had a 2018 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 4.21/10, ranking it 142nd globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Eswatini_landscape.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Eswatini_landscape.jpg/220px-Eswatini_landscape.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="103" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Eswatini_landscape.jpg/330px-Eswatini_landscape.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Eswatini_landscape.jpg/440px-Eswatini_landscape.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2277" data-file-height="1062" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Eswatini_landscape.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Landscape in Eswatini</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_Eswatini" class="mw-redirect" title="Climate of Eswatini">Climate of Eswatini</a></div>
<p>Eswatini is divided into four climatic regions: the Highveld, Middleveld, Lowveld, and Lubombo plateau. Generally speaking, rain falls mostly during the summer months (December to March), often in the form of thunderstorms.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Winter is the <a href="/wiki/Dry_season" title="Dry season">dry season</a>. Annual rainfall is highest on the Highveld in the west, between 1,000 and 2,000&#160;mm (39.4 and 78.7&#160;in). The further east, the less rain, with the Lowveld recording 500 to 900&#160;mm (19.7 to 35.4&#160;in) per annum.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Variations in temperature are also related to the altitude of the different regions. The Highveld temperature is temperate and seldom uncomfortably hot, while the Lowveld may record temperatures around 40&#160;°C (104&#160;°F) in summer.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The average temperatures at Mbabane, according to the season:
</p>
<table class="wikitable">

<tbody><tr>
<td>Spring</td>
<td>September–October</td>
<td>18&#160;°C (64.4&#160;°F)
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td>Summer</td>
<td>November–March</td>
<td>20&#160;°C (68&#160;°F)
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td>Autumn</td>
<td>April–May</td>
<td>17&#160;°C (62.6&#160;°F)
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td>Winter</td>
<td>June–August</td>
<td>13&#160;°C (55.4&#160;°F)
</td></tr></tbody></table><p> The government of Eswatini has expressed concern that climate change is exacerbating existing social challenges such as poverty, a high HIV prevalence, and food insecurity and will drastically restrict the country's ability to develop, as per Vision 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-www4.unfccc.int_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-www4.unfccc.int-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> Economically, climate change has already adversely impacted Eswatini. For instance, the 2015–16 drought decreased sugar and soft drink concentrate production export (Eswatini's largest economic export). Many of Eswatini's major exports are raw, agricultural products and are therefore vulnerable to a changing climate.<sup id="cite_ref-www4.unfccc.int_61-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-www4.unfccc.int-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Biodiversity_and_conservation">Biodiversity and conservation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Biodiversity and conservation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Wildlife_of_Eswatini" title="Wildlife of Eswatini">Wildlife of Eswatini</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:%D7%92%D7%A8%D7%95%D7%99%D7%94_%D7%A9%D7%A2%D7%99%D7%A8%D7%94_-_Grewia_villosa.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/%D7%92%D7%A8%D7%95%D7%99%D7%94_%D7%A9%D7%A2%D7%99%D7%A8%D7%94_-_Grewia_villosa.jpg/170px-%D7%92%D7%A8%D7%95%D7%99%D7%94_%D7%A9%D7%A2%D7%99%D7%A8%D7%94_-_Grewia_villosa.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="220" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/%D7%92%D7%A8%D7%95%D7%99%D7%94_%D7%A9%D7%A2%D7%99%D7%A8%D7%94_-_Grewia_villosa.jpg/255px-%D7%92%D7%A8%D7%95%D7%99%D7%94_%D7%A9%D7%A2%D7%99%D7%A8%D7%94_-_Grewia_villosa.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/%D7%92%D7%A8%D7%95%D7%99%D7%94_%D7%A9%D7%A2%D7%99%D7%A8%D7%94_-_Grewia_villosa.jpg/340px-%D7%92%D7%A8%D7%95%D7%99%D7%94_%D7%A9%D7%A2%D7%99%D7%A8%D7%94_-_Grewia_villosa.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2400" data-file-height="3105" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:%D7%92%D7%A8%D7%95%D7%99%D7%94_%D7%A9%D7%A2%D7%99%D7%A8%D7%94_-_Grewia_villosa.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i><a href="/wiki/Grewia_villosa" title="Grewia villosa">Grewia villosa</a></i></div></div></div>
<p>Eswatini has a spectrum of formal and informal conservation areas that protect the nation's rich biological diversity. These areas comprise about 5% of the country's land area. Eswatini has over 820 species of vertebrates and over 2400 species of plants, with many <a href="/wiki/Endemism" title="Endemism">endemic</a> species. This diversity suggests Eswatini is globally important for biodiversity conservation.<sup id="cite_ref-BSAP_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BSAP-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> Land degradation and conversion to other land uses are the major threats to biodiversity, including plantation agriculture (legal and illegal), bush-clearing, the spread of alien and invasive plants, and unsustainable resource harvesting; major land fragmentation is evident.<sup id="cite_ref-BSAP_62-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BSAP-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Eswatini is a signatory to the <a href="/wiki/Convention_on_Biological_Diversity" title="Convention on Biological Diversity">Convention on Biological Diversity</a>, the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species (<a href="/wiki/CITES" title="CITES">CITES</a>), and the United National Framework Convention on Climate Change. There are three main government ministries responsible for national biodiversity management: the Eswatini National Trust Commission, the <a href="/wiki/Eswatini_Environment_Authority" title="Eswatini Environment Authority">Eswatini Environment Authority</a>, and the Ministry of Agriculture and Cooperatives. In addition, Big Game Parks, a private entity, is tasked with the management of the Game Act, which controls wildlife and CITES.<sup id="cite_ref-BSAP_62-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BSAP-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are 6 formal and more than 10 informal protected areas in the country. The formally gazetted areas include: <a href="/wiki/Malolotja_National_Park" title="Malolotja National Park">Malolotja Nature Reserve</a>, Mantenga Nature Reserve, <a href="/wiki/Mlawula_Nature_Reserve" title="Mlawula Nature Reserve">Mlawula Nature Reserve</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mlilwane_Wildlife_Sanctuary" title="Mlilwane Wildlife Sanctuary">Mlilwane Wildlife Sanctuary</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Mkhaya_Game_Reserve" title="Mkhaya Game Reserve">Mkhaya Game Reserve</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Hlane_Royal_National_Park" title="Hlane Royal National Park">Hlane Royal National Park</a>. In addition to these, there are many private and community nature reserves, as well as some with mixed governance structures. These include: Dombeya Game Reserve, <a href="/wiki/Mbuluzi_Game_Reserve" title="Mbuluzi Game Reserve">Mbuluzi Game Reserve</a>, Shewula Nature Reserve, <a href="/wiki/Phophonyane_Falls_Nature_Reserve" title="Phophonyane Falls Nature Reserve">Phophonyane Falls Nature Reserve</a>, <a href="/wiki/Royal_Jozini" title="Royal Jozini">Royal Jozini</a>, IYSIS (Inyoni Yami), <a href="/wiki/Ngwempisi" title="Ngwempisi">Ngwempisi</a> Wilderness, <a href="/wiki/Sibebe" title="Sibebe">Sibebe</a> and others. There are other entities that practice secondary or tertiary conservation, as well as two conservancies: the Mhlosinga Conservancy and the <a href="/wiki/Lubombo_Conservancy" title="Lubombo Conservancy">Lubombo Conservancy</a>. Others include: the Natural History Society of Eswatini and the Eswatini Game Ranchers Association.
</p><p>From 2014 to 2021, Eswatini participated in the "Strengthening the National Protected Areas System" (SNPAS) project. This project attempted to strengthen conservation outcomes and the national footprint of biodiversity conservation across the country.<sup id="cite_ref-UNDP_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNDP-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> In an effort to broaden the spectrum of areas eligible for conservation support (which practice bona-fide conservation management), the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Programme" title="United Nations Development Programme">United Nations Development Programme</a> (UNDP) established a new category for informal, or non-gazetted, conservation areas in 2018. These are now called OECMs, or Other Effective Conservation Measures. The SNPAS Project adopted this OECM terminology and began certifying informal conservation areas in Eswatini in 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-UNDP_SNPAS_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNDP_SNPAS-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are known to be <a href="/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Eswatini" title="List of birds of Eswatini">507 bird species</a> in Eswatini, including 11 globally threatened species and four introduced species, and <a href="/wiki/List_of_mammals_of_Eswatini" title="List of mammals of Eswatini">107 mammal species</a> native to Eswatini, including the critically endangered <a href="/wiki/South-central_black_rhinoceros" title="South-central black rhinoceros">South-central black rhinoceros</a> and seven other endangered or vulnerable species.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Eswatini is rich in bird life, including <a href="/wiki/White-backed_vulture" title="White-backed vulture">white-backed vultures</a>, <a href="/wiki/White-headed_vulture" title="White-headed vulture">white-headed</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lappet-faced_vulture" title="Lappet-faced vulture">lappet-faced</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cape_vulture" title="Cape vulture">Cape vultures</a>, raptors such as <a href="/wiki/Martial_eagle" title="Martial eagle">martial eagles</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bateleur" title="Bateleur">bateleurs</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Long-crested_eagle" title="Long-crested eagle">long-crested eagles</a>, and the southernmost nesting site of the <a href="/wiki/Marabou_stork" title="Marabou stork">marabou stork</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bgp_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bgp-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Eswatini" title="Government of Eswatini">Government of Eswatini</a> and <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Eswatini" title="Politics of Eswatini">Politics of Eswatini</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mswati_III_King_of_Eswatini.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Mswati_III_King_of_Eswatini.jpg/170px-Mswati_III_King_of_Eswatini.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="254" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Mswati_III_King_of_Eswatini.jpg/255px-Mswati_III_King_of_Eswatini.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Mswati_III_King_of_Eswatini.jpg/340px-Mswati_III_King_of_Eswatini.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1296" data-file-height="1936" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mswati_III_King_of_Eswatini.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Mswati_III" title="Mswati III">Mswati III</a> has been king of Eswatini since 1986.</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Monarchy">Monarchy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Monarchy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Eswatini is an <a href="/wiki/Absolute_monarchy" title="Absolute monarchy">absolute monarchy</a> with constitutional provision and Swazi law and customs.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> is the king or <i><a href="/wiki/Ngwenyama" title="Ngwenyama">ngwenyama</a></i> (lit. <i>lion</i>), currently King Mswati III, who ascended to the throne in 1986 after the death of his father King Sobhuza II in 1982 and a period of regency. According to the country's constitution, the <a href="/wiki/Ngwenyama" title="Ngwenyama">ngwenyama</a> is a symbol of unity and the eternity of the Swazi nation.<sup id="cite_ref-Const05_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Const05-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> By tradition, the king reigns along with his mother (or a ritual substitute), the <i><a href="/wiki/Ndlovukati" title="Ndlovukati">ndlovukati</a></i> (lit. <i>she-</i>e<i>lephant</i>). The former was viewed as the administrative head of state and the latter as a spiritual and national head of state, with real power counterbalancing that of the king, but during the long reign of Sobhuza II, the role of the <i>ndlovukati</i> became more symbolic.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The king appoints the prime minister from the legislature and also appoints a minority of legislators to both chambers of the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Eswatini" title="Parliament of Eswatini">Libandla</a> (parliament) with help from an advisory council. The king is allowed by the constitution to appoint some members to parliament to represent special interests. These special interests are citizens who might have been electoral candidates who were not elected, or might not have stood as candidates. This is done to balance views in parliament. Special interests could be people of particular gender or race, people of disability, the business community, civic society, scholars, and chiefs.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Parliament">Parliament</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Parliament">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Swazi <a href="/wiki/Bicameralism" title="Bicameralism">bicameral</a> Parliament, or Libandla, consists of the <a href="/wiki/Senate_of_Eswatini" title="Senate of Eswatini">Senate</a> (30 seats; 10 members appointed by the House of Assembly and 20 appointed by the monarch; to serve five-year terms) and the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Assembly_of_Eswatini" title="House of Assembly of Eswatini">House of Assembly</a> (65 seats; 10 members appointed by the monarch and 55 elected by <a href="/wiki/Direct_election" title="Direct election">popular vote</a>; to serve five-year terms). The elections are held every five years after dissolution of parliament by the king. The last elections were held on 18 August and 21 September 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> The balloting is done in a <a href="/wiki/Nonpartisanism" title="Nonpartisanism">non-partisan</a> manner. All election procedures are overseen by the Elections and Boundaries Commission.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_culture">Political culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Political culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>At Swaziland's independence on 6 September 1968, Swaziland adopted a <a href="/wiki/Westminster_system" title="Westminster system">Westminster-style</a> constitution. On 12 April 1973, King Sobhuza II annulled it by decree, assuming supreme powers in all executive, judicial, and legislative matters.<sup id="cite_ref-Eisa08_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eisa08-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> The first non-party elections for the House of Assembly were held in 1978, and they were conducted under the <a href="/wiki/Inkhundla" title="Inkhundla">tinkhundla</a> as electoral constituencies determined by the King, and established an Electoral Committee appointed by the King to supervise elections.<sup id="cite_ref-Eisa08_74-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eisa08-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Until the 1993 election, the ballot was not secret, voters were not registered, and they did not elect representatives directly. Instead, voters elected an <a href="/wiki/Electoral_college" title="Electoral college">electoral college</a> by passing through a gate designated for the candidate of choice while officials counted them.<sup id="cite_ref-Eisa08_74-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eisa08-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> Later on, a constitutional review commission was appointed by King Mswati III in July 1996, comprising chiefs, political activists, and unionists to consider public submissions and draft proposals for a new constitution.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Drafts were released for comment in May 1999 and November 2000. These were strongly criticised by civil society organisations in Swaziland and human rights organisations elsewhere. A 15-member team was announced in December 2001 to draft a new constitution; several members of this team were reported to be close to the royal family.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Elections">Elections</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Elections">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Elections_in_Eswatini" title="Elections in Eswatini">Elections in Eswatini</a></div>
<p>Nominations take place at the chiefdoms. On the day of nomination, the name of the nominee is raised by a show of hand, and the nominee either accepts or rejects the nomination. If accepted, the nominee must have the support of at least ten members of that chiefdom. The nominations are for the position of Member of Parliament, Constituency Headman (<i>Indvuna</i>), and the Constituency Executive Committee (<i>Bucopho</i>). The minimum number of nominees is four and the maximum is ten.<sup id="cite_ref-EISA_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EISA-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Primary elections also take place at the chiefdom level. It is by <a href="/wiki/Secret_ballot" title="Secret ballot">secret ballot</a>. During the primary elections, the voters are given an opportunity to elect the member of the executive committee (bucopho) for that particular chiefdom. Aspiring members of parliament and the constituency headman are also elected from each chiefdom. The secondary and final elections takes place at the various constituencies called <a href="/wiki/Inkhundla" title="Inkhundla">tinkhundla</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-EISA_77-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EISA-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> Candidates who win primary elections in the chiefdoms are considered nominees for the secondary elections at inkhundla or constituency level. The nominees with majority votes become the winners and they become members of parliament or constituency headman.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Eswatini" title="Foreign relations of Eswatini">Foreign relations of Eswatini</a></div>
<p>Eswatini is a member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth of Nations</a>, the <a href="/wiki/African_Union" title="African Union">African Union</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Common_Market_for_Eastern_and_Southern_Africa" title="Common Market for Eastern and Southern Africa">Common Market for Eastern and Southern Africa</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Southern_African_Development_Community" title="Southern African Development Community">Southern African Development Community</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> As of 2019, it is the only country in Africa that has maintained ties with <a href="/wiki/Taiwan" title="Taiwan">Taiwan</a> and not the <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">People's Republic of China</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Judiciary">Judiciary</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Judiciary">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The judicial system in Eswatini is a dual system. The 2005 constitution established a court system based on the <a href="/wiki/Western_world" title="Western world">Western</a> model consisting of four regional Magistrates Courts, a High Court, and a Court of Appeal (the Supreme Court), which are independent of crown control. In addition, traditional courts (Swazi Courts or Customary Courts) deal with minor offenses and violations of traditional Swazi law and custom.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Judges are appointed by the king and are usually expatriates from South Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> The Supreme Court, which replaced the previous Court of Appeal, consists of the chief justice and at least four other Supreme Court judges. The High Court consists of the chief justice and at least four High Court judges.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> The chief justices have been:
</p>
<ul><li>1967–1970: Sir Isadore Victor Elgan</li>
<li>1970-1972: Sir <a href="/wiki/Philip_Pike" title="Philip Pike">Philip Pike</a><sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>1972-1973-?: Roland Hill</li>
<li>?-1974-1983-?: Charles Nathan</li>
<li>1985-1991: Nicholas Robin Hannah<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>1998–2002: Stanley Sapire</li>
<li>2002–2007: Jacobus Annandale (acting)</li>
<li>2007–2010: <a href="/wiki/Richard_Banda" title="Richard Banda">Richard Banda</a><sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>2010–2015: <a href="/wiki/Michael_Ramodibedi" title="Michael Ramodibedi">Michael Ramodibedi</a><sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>2015–present: <a href="/wiki/Bheki_Maphalala" title="Bheki Maphalala">Bheki Maphalala</a><sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Rangers_in_Action_22-African_Land_Forces_Summit-US_Army_Africa-13_MAY_2010.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Rangers_in_Action_22-African_Land_Forces_Summit-US_Army_Africa-13_MAY_2010.jpg/220px-Rangers_in_Action_22-African_Land_Forces_Summit-US_Army_Africa-13_MAY_2010.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Rangers_in_Action_22-African_Land_Forces_Summit-US_Army_Africa-13_MAY_2010.jpg/330px-Rangers_in_Action_22-African_Land_Forces_Summit-US_Army_Africa-13_MAY_2010.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Rangers_in_Action_22-African_Land_Forces_Summit-US_Army_Africa-13_MAY_2010.jpg/440px-Rangers_in_Action_22-African_Land_Forces_Summit-US_Army_Africa-13_MAY_2010.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2494" data-file-height="1669" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Rangers_in_Action_22-African_Land_Forces_Summit-US_Army_Africa-13_MAY_2010.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Swazi army officers</div></div></div>
<p>The military of Eswatini (<a href="/wiki/Umbutfo_Eswatini_Defence_Force" title="Umbutfo Eswatini Defence Force">Umbutfo Eswatini Defence Force</a>) is used primarily during domestic protests, with some border and customs duties. The military has never been involved in a foreign conflict.<sup id="cite_ref-crash_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-crash-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> The king is the <a href="/wiki/Commander-in-chief" title="Commander-in-chief">commander-in-chief</a> of the defence force and the substantive Minister of the Ministry of Defence.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> There are approximately 3,000 personnel in the defence force, with the army being the largest component.<sup id="cite_ref-sipri1_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sipri1-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> There is a small air force, which is mainly used for transporting the king as well as cargo and personnel, surveying land with search and rescue functions, and mobilising in case of a national emergency.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="floatright"><div class="noresize" style="height: 246px; width: 200px; "><map name="ImageMap_44e498b8fedd1ad9"><area href="/wiki/Hhohho_Region" shape="rect" coords="57,48,116,71" alt="Hhohho Region" title="Hhohho Region" /><area href="/wiki/Lubombo_Region" shape="rect" coords="121,113,180,131" alt="Lubombo Region" title="Lubombo Region" /><area href="/wiki/Manzini_Region" shape="rect" coords="40,121,96,141" alt="Manzini Region" title="Manzini Region" /><area href="/wiki/Shiselweni_Region" shape="rect" coords="59,182,129,203" alt="Shiselweni Region" title="Shiselweni Region" /></map><img alt="A clickable map of Eswatini exhibiting its four districts." src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/Eswatini%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored.png/200px-Eswatini%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored.png" decoding="async" width="200" height="246" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/Eswatini%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored.png/300px-Eswatini%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/Eswatini%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored.png/400px-Eswatini%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored.png 2x" data-file-width="732" data-file-height="899" usemap="#ImageMap_44e498b8fedd1ad9" /><div style="margin-left: 0px; margin-top: -20px; text-align: left;"><a href="/wiki/File:Eswatini,_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_colored.png" title="About this image"><img alt="About this image" src="/w/extensions/ImageMap/resources/desc-20.png?15600" style="border: none;" /></a></div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Regions_of_Eswatini" title="Regions of Eswatini">Regions of Eswatini</a></div>
<p>Eswatini is divided into four regions: <a href="/wiki/Hhohho_Region" title="Hhohho Region">Hhohho</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lubombo_Region" title="Lubombo Region">Lubombo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Manzini_Region" title="Manzini Region">Manzini</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Shiselweni_Region" title="Shiselweni Region">Shiselweni</a>. In each of the four regions, there are several <i>tinkhundla</i> (singular <i>inkhundla</i>). The regions are managed by a regional administrator, who is aided by elected members in each inkhundla.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> The local government is divided into differently structured rural and urban councils depending on the level of development in the area. Although there are different political structures to the local authorities, effectively the urban councils are municipalities and the rural councils are the tinkhundla. There are twelve municipalities and 55 tinkhundla.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Each inkhundla has a development committee (<i>bucopho</i>) elected from the various constituency chiefdoms in its area for a five-year term. Bucopho bring to the inkhundla all matters of interest and concern to their various chiefdoms, and take back to the chiefdoms the decisions of the inkhundla. The chairman of the bucopho is elected at the inkhundla and is called <i>indvuna ye nkhundla</i>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>There are three tiers of government in the urban areas and these are city councils, town councils and town boards. This variation considers the size of the town or city. Equally, there are three tiers in the rural areas which are the regional administration at the regional level, tinkhundla and chiefdoms. Decisions are made by full council based on recommendations made by the various sub-committees. The town clerk is the chief advisor in each local council or town board.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> There are twelve declared urban areas, comprising two city councils, three town councils and seven town boards. The main cities and towns in Eswatini are <a href="/wiki/Manzini,_Swaziland" class="mw-redirect" title="Manzini, Swaziland">Manzini</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mbabane" title="Mbabane">Mbabane</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nhlangano" title="Nhlangano">Nhlangano</a> and <a href="/wiki/Siteki" title="Siteki">Siteki</a> which are also regional capitals.
</p>
