<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:332px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Swaziland_treemap.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Swaziland_treemap.png/330px-Swaziland_treemap.png" decoding="async" width="330" height="258" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Swaziland_treemap.png/495px-Swaziland_treemap.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Swaziland_treemap.png/660px-Swaziland_treemap.png 2x" data-file-width="1107" data-file-height="865" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Swaziland_treemap.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Swazi exports</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Eswatini" title="Economy of Eswatini">Eswatini's economy</a> is diverse, with agriculture, forestry and mining accounting for about 13% of GDP, manufacturing (textiles and sugar-related processing) representing 37% of GDP and services&#160;– with government services in the lead&#160;– constituting 50% of GDP. <a href="/wiki/Land_tenure" title="Land tenure">Title Deed</a> Lands (TDLs), where the bulk of high value crops are grown (sugar, forestry, and citrus), are characterised by high levels of investment and irrigation, and high productivity.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> About 75% of the population is employed in <a href="/wiki/Subsistence_agriculture" title="Subsistence agriculture">subsistence agriculture</a> upon <a href="/wiki/Swazi_Nation_Land" title="Swazi Nation Land">Swazi Nation Land</a> (SNL). In contrast with the commercial farms, SNL suffers from low productivity and investment.
</p><p>The cultivation of <a href="/wiki/Sugarcane" title="Sugarcane">sugarcane</a>, the country's main resource, enslaves part of the population: forced evictions of rural communities to build plantations, child labour, work weeks of up to 60 hours, etc. The <a href="/wiki/International_Trade_Union_Confederation" title="International Trade Union Confederation">International Trade Union Confederation</a> refers to "arduous and unhealthy working conditions, miserable wages and violent repression of any attempt to unionize."<sup id="cite_ref-mon_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mon-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> Economic growth has lagged behind that of neighbouring countries. Real GDP growth since 2001 has averaged 2.8%, nearly 2 percentage points lower than growth in other <a href="/wiki/Southern_African_Customs_Union" title="Southern African Customs Union">Southern African Customs Union</a> (SACU) member countries. Low agricultural productivity in the SNLs, repeated droughts, the devastating effect of HIV/AIDS and an overly large and inefficient government sector are likely contributing factors. Eswatini's public finances deteriorated in the late 1990s following sizeable surpluses a decade earlier. A combination of declining revenues and increased spending led to significant budget deficits.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Central_Bank_of_Eswatini.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/6/68/Central_Bank_of_Eswatini.jpg/220px-Central_Bank_of_Eswatini.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="139" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/6/68/Central_Bank_of_Eswatini.jpg/330px-Central_Bank_of_Eswatini.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/6/68/Central_Bank_of_Eswatini.jpg/440px-Central_Bank_of_Eswatini.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1288" data-file-height="813" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Central_Bank_of_Eswatini.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Central Bank in Mbabane</div></div></div>
<p>Eswatini's economy is very closely linked to the <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_South_Africa" title="Economy of South Africa">economy of South Africa</a>, from which it receives over 90% of its imports and to which it sends about 70% of its exports. Eswatini's other key trading partners are the United States  (under the <a href="/wiki/African_Growth_and_Opportunity_Act" title="African Growth and Opportunity Act">African Growth and Opportunity Act</a>) and the EU, from whom the country has received trade preferences for apparel exports to the U.S. and for sugar to the EU. Under these agreements, both apparel and sugar exports did well, with rapid growth and a strong inflow of foreign direct investment.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Southern_African_Customs_Union.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Southern_African_Customs_Union.svg/220px-Southern_African_Customs_Union.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="220" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Southern_African_Customs_Union.svg/330px-Southern_African_Customs_Union.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Southern_African_Customs_Union.svg/440px-Southern_African_Customs_Union.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="1000" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Southern_African_Customs_Union.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Eswatini is part of the <a href="/wiki/Southern_African_Customs_Union" title="Southern African Customs Union">Southern African Customs Union</a> (green).</div></div></div>
<p>The continued vibrancy of the export sector is threatened by the removal of trade preferences for textiles, the accession to similar preferences for East Asian countries, and the phasing out of preferential prices for sugar to the EU market. Eswatini will thus have to face the challenge of remaining competitive in a changing global environment. The Investment Climate Assessment provides some positive findings, namely that Eswatini firms are among the most productive in Sub-Saharan Africa, although they are less productive than firms in the most productive middle-income countries in other regions. They compare more favourably with firms from lower middle income countries but are hampered by inadequate governance arrangements and infrastructure.<sup id="cite_ref-Eswatini_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eswatini-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Eswatini's currency, the <a href="/wiki/Swazi_lilangeni" title="Swazi lilangeni">lilangeni</a>, is pegged to the <a href="/wiki/South_African_rand" title="South African rand">South African rand</a>, subsuming Eswatini's monetary policy to South Africa. Customs duties from the Southern African Customs Union and worker <a href="/wiki/Remittance" title="Remittance">remittances</a> from South Africa substantially supplement domestically earned income. Eswatini is not poor enough to merit an <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">IMF</a> programme; however, the country is struggling to reduce the size of the civil service and control costs at public enterprises. The government is trying to improve the atmosphere for <a href="/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment" title="Foreign direct investment">foreign direct investment</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Eswatini_102-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eswatini-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Public services are very poorly developed: the country has only twelve public ambulances, elementary schools generally no longer provide canteens and pharmacies are disappearing.<sup id="cite_ref-mon_101-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mon-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>An economic circle of 15,000 businessmen takes most of the country's wealth. This circle includes South African investors who have come to Eswatini to find a workforce that is three times cheaper and a group of white businessmen who are heirs to the British settlers.<sup id="cite_ref-mon_101-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mon-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> King Mswati III receives 8% of the national budget for official expenses. The police force receives 5% of the budget, as do the armed forces.<sup id="cite_ref-mon_101-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mon-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Eswatini" title="Demographics of Eswatini">Demographics of Eswatini</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:352px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Eswatini-demography.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Eswatini-demography.svg/350px-Eswatini-demography.svg.png" decoding="async" width="350" height="233" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Eswatini-demography.svg/525px-Eswatini-demography.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Eswatini-demography.svg/700px-Eswatini-demography.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1080" data-file-height="720" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Eswatini-demography.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Eswatini's population in thousands (1950–2021)</div></div></div>
<p>The majority of Eswatini's population is ethnically <a href="/wiki/Swazi_people" title="Swazi people">Swazi</a>, mixed with a small number of <a href="/wiki/Zulu_people" title="Zulu people">Zulu</a> and <a href="/wiki/White_Africans_of_European_ancestry" title="White Africans of European ancestry">White Africans</a>, mostly people of British and <a href="/wiki/Afrikaners" title="Afrikaners">Afrikaner</a> descent. Traditionally Swazi have been subsistence farmers and herders, but most now mix such activities with work in the growing urban formal economy and in government. Some Swazi work in the mines in South Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> Eswatini also received <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_people" title="Portuguese people">Portuguese</a> settlers and African refugees from Mozambique. Christianity in Eswatini is sometimes mixed with traditional beliefs and practices. Many traditionalists believe that most Swazi ascribe a special spiritual role to the monarch.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Eswatini" title="Languages of Eswatini">Languages of Eswatini</a></div>
<p><i><a href="/wiki/SiSwati_language" class="mw-redirect" title="SiSwati language">SiSwati</a></i><sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> (also known as <i>Swati</i>, <i>Swazi</i> or <i>Siswati</i>) is a <a href="/wiki/Bantu_languages" title="Bantu languages">Bantu language</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Nguni_languages" title="Nguni languages">Nguni</a> Group, spoken in Eswatini and South Africa. It has 2.5&#160;million speakers and is taught in schools. It is an official language of Eswatini, along with English,<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> and one of the official languages of South Africa. English is the medium of communication in schools, conducting business, and the press.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> About 76,000&#160;people in the country speak <a href="/wiki/Zulu_language" title="Zulu language">Zulu</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Tsonga_language" title="Tsonga language">Tsonga</a>, which is spoken by many people throughout the region is spoken by about 19,000&#160;people in Eswatini. <a href="/wiki/Afrikaans" title="Afrikaans">Afrikaans</a> is also spoken by some residents of Afrikaner descent. Portuguese has been introduced as a third language in the schools because of the large community of Portuguese speakers from Mozambique<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> or <a href="/wiki/North_Region,_Portugal" title="North Region, Portugal">Northern</a> and <a href="/wiki/Central_Region,_Portugal" title="Central Region, Portugal">Central Portugal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Eswatini" title="Religion in Eswatini">Religion in Eswatini</a></div>
<p>Eighty-three percent of the total population adheres to Christianity in Eswatini. <a href="/wiki/Anglicanism" title="Anglicanism">Anglican</a>, <a href="/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism">Protestant</a> and <a href="/wiki/African-initiated_church" title="African-initiated church">indigenous African churches</a>, including <a href="/wiki/African_Zionism" title="African Zionism">African Zionist</a> (40%), constitute the majority of Christians, followed by Roman Catholicism at 6% of the population. On 18 July 2012, <a href="/wiki/Ellinah_Wamukoya" title="Ellinah Wamukoya">Ellinah Wamukoya</a>, was elected <i>Anglican Bishop of Swaziland</i>, becoming the first woman to be a bishop in Africa and serving in that position until her death in 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> Fifteen percent of the population follows <a href="/wiki/Traditional_African_religions" title="Traditional African religions">traditional religions</a><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>; other non-Christian religions practised in the country include <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> (2%<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>), the <a href="/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith" title="Baháʼí Faith">Baháʼí Faith</a> (0.5%), and <a href="/wiki/Hinduism" title="Hinduism">Hinduism</a> (0.2%).<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> There were 14 <a href="/wiki/Jews" title="Jews">Jewish</a> families in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Kingdom of Eswatini does not recognise non-civil marriages such as Islamic-rite marriage contracts.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Eswatini" title="Health in Eswatini">Health in Eswatini</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS_in_Eswatini" title="HIV/AIDS in Eswatini">HIV/AIDS in Eswatini</a></div>
<p>As of 2019, Eswatini has the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_HIV/AIDS_adult_prevalence_rate" title="List of countries by HIV/AIDS adult prevalence rate">highest prevalence of HIV</a> among people aged 15 to 49 in the world (27.1%).<sup id="cite_ref-unaids16_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unaids16-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:242px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Rural_primary_school_in_Eswatini.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Rural_primary_school_in_Eswatini.jpg/240px-Rural_primary_school_in_Eswatini.jpg" decoding="async" width="240" height="180" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Rural_primary_school_in_Eswatini.jpg/360px-Rural_primary_school_in_Eswatini.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Rural_primary_school_in_Eswatini.jpg/480px-Rural_primary_school_in_Eswatini.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="480" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Rural_primary_school_in_Eswatini.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A rural primary school in Eswatini</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Education_in_Eswatini" title="Education in Eswatini">Education in Eswatini</a> begins with pre-school education for infants, primary, secondary and high school education for general education and training, and universities and colleges at the tertiary level. Pre-school education is usually for children 5-years or younger; after that, a student can enroll in a primary school anywhere in the country. Early childhood care and education centres take the form of preschools or neighbourhood care points. In the country 21.6% of preschool age children have access to early childhood education.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> Primary education begins at age six. It is a seven-year programme that culminates with an end-of-primary-school examination in grade 7 which is a locally based assessment administered by the Examinations Council through schools.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The secondary and high school education system is a five-year programme divided into three years junior secondary and two years senior secondary. There is an external public examination (Junior Certificate) at the end of the junior secondary that learners must pass to progress to the senior secondary level. The Examinations Council of Swaziland administers this examination. At the end of the senior secondary level, learners sit for a public examination, the Swaziland General Certificate of Secondary Education and International General Certificate of Secondary Education which is accredited by the Cambridge International Examination. A few schools offer the Advanced Studies programme in their curriculum.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are 830 public schools including primary, secondary and high schools.<sup id="cite_ref-SZEDSchoolList_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SZEDSchoolList-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> There are also 34 recognised private schools with an additional 14 unrecognised private schools. The largest number of schools is in the Hhohho region.<sup id="cite_ref-SZEDSchoolList_120-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SZEDSchoolList-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> Education is free at primary level, mainly first through the fourth grade and also free for orphaned and vulnerable children, but not compulsory.<sup id="cite_ref-ilab_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilab-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> In 1996, the net primary school enrollment rate was 90.8%, with gender parity at the primary level.<sup id="cite_ref-ilab_121-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilab-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> In 1998, 80.5% of children reached grade five.<sup id="cite_ref-ilab_121-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilab-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1963, Waterford School, later named <a href="/wiki/Waterford_Kamhlaba" title="Waterford Kamhlaba">Waterford Kamhlaba United World College of Southern Africa</a>, was founded as southern Africa's first multiracial school. In 1981, Waterford Kamhlaba joined the <a href="/wiki/United_World_Colleges" title="United World Colleges">United World Colleges</a> movement as the first United World College on the African continent, and the only African UWC until 2019 when UWC East Africa was established.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Higher_education">Higher education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Higher education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/University_of_Eswatini" title="University of Eswatini">University of Eswatini</a>, Southern African Nazarene University and Eswatini Medical Christian  University are the institutions that offer university education in the country. A campus of <a href="/wiki/Limkokwing_University_of_Creative_Technology" title="Limkokwing University of Creative Technology">Limkokwing University of Creative Technology</a> can be found at Sidvwashini (Sidwashini), a suburb of Mbabane. Ngwane Teacher's College and William Pitcher College are the country's teaching colleges. The Good Shepherd Hospital in <a href="/wiki/Siteki" title="Siteki">Siteki</a> is home to the College for Nursing Assistants.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Klopper12_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Klopper12-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> The University of Eswatini is the national university, established in 1982 by act of Parliament, and is headquartered at <a href="/wiki/Kwaluseni" title="Kwaluseni">Kwaluseni</a> with additional campuses in Mbabane and <a href="/wiki/Luyengo" title="Luyengo">Luyengo</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> The Southern African Nazarene University in Manzini was established in 2010 as a merger of the Nazarene College of Nursing, College of Theology and the Nazarene Teachers College.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Eswatini Medical Christian University,<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> focusing on medical education, was established in 2012 and is Eswatini's newest university.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> It is in Mbabane.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> The campus of Limkokwing University was opened at Sidvwashini in Mbabane in 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> The main centre for technical training in Eswatini is the <a href="/wiki/Eswatini_College_of_Technology" title="Eswatini College of Technology">Eswatini College of Technology</a><sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> Other technical and vocational institutions include the <a href="/wiki/Gwamile_Vocational_and_Commercial_Training_Institute" title="Gwamile Vocational and Commercial Training Institute">Gwamile Vocational and Commercial Training Institute</a> in <a href="/wiki/Matsapha" title="Matsapha">Matsapha</a>, the Manzini Industrial and Training Centre in Manzini, Nhlangano Agricultural Skills Training Centre, and Siteki Industrial Training Centre.
</p><p>In addition to these institutions, the kingdom also has the Eswatini Institute of Management and Public Administration (SIMPA)<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> and Institute of Development Management (IDM). SIMPA is a government-owned management and development institute, and IDM is a regional organisation in Botswana, Lesotho, and Eswatini, providing training, consultancy, and research in management. North Carolina State University's <a href="/wiki/Poole_College_of_Management" title="Poole College of Management">Poole College of Management</a> is a sister school of SIMPA.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> The Mananga Management Centre was established at <a href="/wiki/Ezulwini_Valley" title="Ezulwini Valley">Ezulwini</a> as Mananga Agricultural Management Centre in 1972 as an international management development centre offering training of middle and senior managers.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Eswatini" title="Culture of Eswatini">Culture of Eswatini</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Eswatini" title="Music of Eswatini">Music of Eswatini</a></div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+link+.ambox{margin-top:-1px}html body.mediawiki .mw-parser-output .ambox.mbox-small-left{margin:4px 1em 4px .ambox-delete{border-left:10px solid #b32424}.mw-parser-output .ambox-content{border-left:10px solid #f28500}.mw-parser-output .ambox-style{border-left:10px solid #fc3}.mw-parser-output .ambox-move{border-left:10px solid #9932cc}.mw-parser-output .ambox-protection{border-left:10px solid #a2a9b1}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-text{border:none;padding:0.25em .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-Unreferenced_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit">improve Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">April 2018</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:King_Mswati_III,_2006.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/King_Mswati_III%2C_2006.jpg/170px-King_Mswati_III%2C_2006.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="255" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/King_Mswati_III%2C_2006.jpg/255px-King_Mswati_III%2C_2006.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/King_Mswati_III%2C_2006.jpg/340px-King_Mswati_III%2C_2006.jpg 2x" data-file-width="959" data-file-height="1439" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:King_Mswati_III,_2006.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>King <a href="/wiki/Mswati_III" title="Mswati III">Mswati III</a> at the reed dance festival where he will choose his next wife</div></div></div>
<p>The principal Swazi social unit is the homestead, a traditional beehive hut thatched with dry grass. In a polygamous homestead, each wife has her own hut and yard surrounded by reed fences. There are three structures for sleeping, cooking, and storage (brewing beer). Larger homesteads also have structures used as bachelors' quarters and guest accommodation. Central to the traditional homestead is the cattle <a href="/wiki/Byre" class="mw-redirect" title="Byre">byre</a>, a circular area enclosed by large logs, interspersed with branches. The cattle byre has ritual as well as practical significance as a store of wealth and symbol of prestige. It contains sealed grain pits. Facing the cattle byre is the great hut which is occupied by the mother of the headman. The headman is central to all homestead affairs, and he is often <a href="/wiki/Polygamy" title="Polygamy">polygamous</a>. He leads through example and advises his wives on all social affairs of the home, as well as seeing to the well-being of the family. He also spends time socialising with the young boys, who are often his sons or close relatives, advising them on the expectations of growing up and manhood.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Traditional_healers_of_Southern_Africa" title="Traditional healers of Southern Africa">sangoma</a> is a traditional diviner chosen by the ancestors of that particular family. The training of the sangoma is called "kwetfwasa". At the end of the training, a graduation ceremony takes place where all the local sangoma come together for feasting and dancing. The diviner is consulted for various purposes, such as determining the cause of sickness or even death. His diagnosis is based on "kubhula", a process of communication, through trance, with the natural superpowers. The inyanga (a medical and pharmaceutical specialist in western terms) possesses the bone throwing skill ("kushaya ematsambo") used to determine the cause of the sickness.
</p><p>The most important cultural event in Eswatini is the <a href="/wiki/Incwala" title="Incwala">Incwala</a> ceremony.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> It is held on the fourth day after the full moon nearest the longest day, 21 December. Incwala is often translated in English as "first fruits ceremony", but the king's tasting of the new harvest is only one aspect among many in this long pageant. Incwala is best translated as "Kingship Ceremony": when there is no king, there is no incwala. It is a crime for any other person to hold an Incwala. Every Swazi may take part in the public parts of the Incwala. The climax of the event is the fourth day of the Big Incwala. The key figures are the king, queen mother, royal wives and children, the royal governors (<a href="/wiki/InDuna" title="InDuna">indunas</a>), the chiefs, the regiments, and the "bemanti" or "water people".
</p><p>Eswatini's most well-known cultural event is the annual <a href="/wiki/Umhlanga_(ceremony)" title="Umhlanga (ceremony)">Umhlanga Reed Dance</a>. In the eight-day ceremony, girls cut reeds, present them to the Queen Mother and then dance. It is done in late August or early September. Only childless, unmarried girls can take part. The aims of the ceremony are to preserve girls' <a href="/wiki/Sexual_abstinence" title="Sexual abstinence">chastity</a>, provide tribute labour for the Queen Mother and to encourage solidarity by working together. The royal family appoints a commoner maiden to be "induna" (captain) of the girls and she announces the dates of the annual ceremony over the radio. The chosen induna is expected to be an expert dancer and knowledgeable on royal protocol. One of the king's daughters acts as her counterpart during the ceremony. The Reed Dance today is not an ancient ceremony but a development of the old "<a href="/wiki/Umchwasho" title="Umchwasho">umchwasho</a>" custom. In "umchwasho", all young girls were placed in a female age-regiment. If any girl became pregnant outside of marriage, her family paid a fine of one cow to the local chief. After a number of years, when the girls had reached a marriageable age, they would perform labour service for the queen mother, ending with dancing and feasting. The country was under the rite of "umchwasho" until 2005.
</p><p>Eswatini is also known for a strong presence in the handcrafts industry. The formalised handcraft businesses of Eswatini employ over 2,500 people, many of whom are women.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> The products are unique and reflect the culture of Eswatini, ranging from housewares, to artistic decorations, to complex glass, stone or wood artwork.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Eswatini&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Sport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Eswatini_at_the_Olympics" title="Eswatini at the Olympics">Eswatini at the Olympics</a> and <a href="/wiki/Eswatini_at_the_Commonwealth_Games" title="Eswatini at the Commonwealth Games">Eswatini at the Commonwealth Games</a></div>
<p>Eswatini has sent athletes to the <a href="/wiki/Summer_Olympics" class="mw-redirect" title="Summer Olympics">Summer Olympics</a> since 1972 but is yet to win a medal. The country has won medals in boxing and marathon at the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_Games" title="Commonwealth Games">Commonwealth Games</a>. Team sports popular in Eswatini include football, cricket and rugby union. The <a href="/wiki/Somhlolo_National_Stadium" title="Somhlolo National Stadium">Somhlolo National Stadium</a> is the largest sporting venue.
</p>
